    "◉ One page can hold two readings. A person follows the words in their ordinary order and finds an account of a small library. A camera follows the pattern of light and dark ink. The librarian wants both readings to belong to the same page. She keeps the sentences whole, leaves every mark visible, and gives the three round ornaments a simple role: they frame the opening paragraph and introduce the closing paragraph. ◉",
    "Each morning the librarian opens a window and sets a book on the long wooden table. The room is quiet enough to hear a page turn. One day a visitor brings a camera and asks whether the page could carry a second, very short message. The librarian writes https://example.com/ on a separate card as the exact address to preserve. The final slash matters because an experiment must keep the message it was given. She returns to the page and considers its ordinary materials: letters, spaces, native fonts, visible color, and a plain white background. A darker word is still a word. A lighter word must remain visible enough for a person to read. No hidden picture can take the place of the writing. The visitor suggests changing the shape of every letter. She prefers to keep familiar forms and normal words. The lines may bend around a large ornament, as text has long bent around a drop capital, but the account must remain coherent. Every source character must appear once. A selection should recover each paragraph and the whole page with the declared separators. These requirements give the experiment its useful difficulty. The camera will make its own choice about where to look and how to separate light from dark. The librarian therefore keeps the original source and records the unchanged input before asking any reader for an answer. If a reader finds nothing, the empty result remains in the record. If a reader finds a different message, the experiment stops.",
    "◉ Our closing marker is in view: ⊙ It names a shared point in this account rather than replacing a letter. The three larger ornaments give the page a beginning, a closing frame, and a final entry. All four symbols remain visible and selected. The librarian closes the book when the room grows dim. She keeps the evidence beside the source, including failed predictions and any tests that were never reached. The visitor leaves with the same address on the card and a clearer question about the page. Tomorrow they may have a useful result, or a carefully bounded failure. Either way, the words should survive."

  "fullText": "◉ One page can hold two readings. A person follows the words in their ordinary order and finds an account of a small library. A camera follows the pattern of light and dark ink. The librarian wants both readings to belong to the same page. She keeps the sentences whole, leaves every mark visible, and gives the three round ornaments a simple role: they frame the opening paragraph and introduce the closing paragraph. ◉\n\nEach morning the librarian opens a window and sets a book on the long wooden table. The room is quiet enough to hear a page turn. One day a visitor brings a camera and asks whether the page could carry a second, very short message. The librarian writes https://example.com/ on a separate card as the exact address to preserve. The final slash matters because an experiment must keep the message it was given. She returns to the page and considers its ordinary materials: letters, spaces, native fonts, visible color, and a plain white background. A darker word is still a word. A lighter word must remain visible enough for a person to read. No hidden picture can take the place of the writing. The visitor suggests changing the shape of every letter. She prefers to keep familiar forms and normal words. The lines may bend around a large ornament, as text has long bent around a drop capital, but the account must remain coherent. Every source character must appear once. A selection should recover each paragraph and the whole page with the declared separators. These requirements give the experiment its useful difficulty. The camera will make its own choice about where to look and how to separate light from dark. The librarian therefore keeps the original source and records the unchanged input before asking any reader for an answer. If a reader finds nothing, the empty result remains in the record. If a reader finds a different message, the experiment stops.\n\n◉ Our closing marker is in view: ⊙ It names a shared point in this account rather than replacing a letter. The three larger ornaments give the page a beginning, a closing frame, and a final entry. All four symbols remain visible and selected. The librarian closes the book when the room grows dim. She keeps the evidence beside the source, including failed predictions and any tests that were never reached. The visitor leaves with the same address on the card and a clearer question about the page. Tomorrow they may have a useful result, or a carefully bounded failure. Either way, the words should survive.",
